# AI Media Scout — 2026-11-24

The edition has 55 complete guides and 16 screened discoveries. It completes 15 earlier pending guides and details 4 substantive release updates. The Nov 24 release of Counterpunch adds assistant-written glyph filters and changes its plugin contracts. CozyClay 3.3 adds a model-written motion specification and contact review, and removes storyboard projects. This edition also looks at dialogue cut handoff, local diarization and model-assisted layout recovery for preserved publications. Code grants were read in full. Models, services, creative inputs and hardware or software hosts have separate requirements. This edition is based on source review and published documentation. No discovered app was installed or run. The expanded plan covered 60 fields, 334/334 repository-query attempts and 21 model tasks. The repository lane recorded 334 successful, 0 partial and 0 failed queries, with 97 bounded. The model lane recorded 0 failed tasks. Metadata from 477 saved repository pages included 19,672 numeric identities. The complete retained backlog spans 123,108 candidates, including pilot history and 1,074 previously screened guides still pending before this edition. Creative relevance and the complete software grant were checked before including any discovery. Unreviewed and excluded records stay in local research. Live primary research yielded 284 complete usable responses from 327 distinct URL attempts, including a non-GitHub source in each of 60 fields. 0 captures reached the size bound and 43 other sources were inaccessible or unusable. The ecosystem checks cover all 8 configured groups and record available findings and gaps. Evidence labels in this edition are 0 Recommended, 0 Ready to try, 59 Quality unverified, 12 Experimental. Repository searches returned no saved rows for Adaptive projection installations, Astronomical imaging and data art, Generative playable worlds, Neural grooming and avatar asset handoff, Learned and editable procedural materials, Semantic neural-scene edits. Primary web research for these fields is included in the coverage notes. Updated evidence labels for previous entries: 3DCraft (Quality unverified to Experimental), ParCAD (Quality unverified to Experimental), Verwrity (Quality unverified to Experimental), Audio as Code (Experimental to Quality unverified). 21 model tasks reached the configured response limit of 50 rows. A model record remains a discovery lead until code and use are verified. The separate watchlist made 66 unsuccessful repository checks. Collection logged 2 enrichment failures. Credential-shaped material in unsealed captures was removed before sealing. The affected source origins and before-and-after hashes remain in private evidence.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Search and review counts

60 creative fields; 334/334 repository queries attempted; 21/21 model-task queries attempted. 21869 distinct source candidates and 978 model leads. 55 detailed profiles and 16 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 97 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Audio as Code

Audio, music & voice · Editable humming and melody arrangement · Optical notation and score editing · Computational art & creative coding

The October 17 v0.5.0 release adds Euclidean rhythms, swing, accents and variations with matched-loudness comparison. It preserves the editable score format.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Make two editable versions of a short music cue and compare swing and accents before transferring MIDI to a workstation.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-24. No local execution implied.

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. A model-written score still needs a musical and timing review. The public before/after demonstration is supplied by the maintainer.

### How it uses AI

A coding assistant can author the structured score and call the project’s validation and rendering tools. The offline engine renders the resulting notes and instrument code to WAV and MIDI. A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/joaoCarvalho1000/audio-as-code/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/joaoCarvalho1000/audio-as-code/releases/tag/v0.5.0) [Source 3](https://pypi.org/pypi/audio-as-code/0.5.0/json) [Source 4](https://audioascode.com/#groove)

1. Author a small rhythmic phrase and render one baseline cue.
2. Change the phrase accents, swing or selected repeats. Render the new cue and compare it at matched loudness.
3. Keep the editable score and export MIDI and WAV as separate handoffs.

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. A project script can mix model services with different output and privacy policies. Preserve modality-specific linkage for accurate cost and rights review.

### How it uses AI

A structured script routes presentation beats to selected speech, imagery and motion backends. Singing beats can drive avatar lip sync from a score instead of ordinary TTS. [Source](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/releases/tag/2.24.0) [Source](https://mulmocast.com/) [Source](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/blob/main/scripts/test/test_avatar_singing.json)

### Introduction

A structured script routes presentation beats to selected speech, imagery and motion backends. Singing beats can drive avatar lip sync from a score instead of ordinary TTS. [Source 1](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/releases/tag/2.24.0) [Source 3](https://mulmocast.com/) [Source 4](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/blob/main/scripts/test/test_avatar_singing.json)

Produce a short narrated or sung presentation with editable beats, captions and clearly identified voice providers. 1. Use the documented Node/npm route and choose the version appropriate to the desired beat schema.
2. Configure only the required model and speech providers. The optional NEUTRINO singing path has a separate local runtime.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/releases/tag/2.24.0) [Source 3](https://mulmocast.com/) [Source 4](https://github.com/receptron/mulmocast-cli/blob/main/scripts/test/test_avatar_singing.json)

1. Write a small script with an introduction and one timed demonstration or sung beat.
2. Generate a preview and check the reporting of used providers, caption timing and the corresponding animation.
3. Revise the original beats and keep the final script and output in the same project archive.

[Source](https://github.com/khawjaahmad/nemotron-diarization-web/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization) [Source](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/Nemotron-3-Diarization-ONNX)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Quantized WebGPU graphs are unsupported and the fp16 graph reportedly produces incorrect decisions on WebGPU. Compare results to careful manual listening.

### How it uses AI

Browser ONNX inference segments live or recorded audio by speaker, including overlapping turns. A worker and a TypeScript speaker cache carry identities across chunks. [Source](https://github.com/khawjaahmad/nemotron-diarization-web/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization) [Source](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/Nemotron-3-Diarization-ONNX)

### Introduction

Browser ONNX inference segments live or recorded audio by speaker, including overlapping turns. A worker and a TypeScript speaker cache carry identities across chunks. ### What it is good for

Review turns and overlap in a short interview or oral-history recording before assigning speakers in an edited transcript. [Source 1](https://github.com/khawjaahmad/nemotron-diarization-web/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/Nemotron-3-Diarization-ONNX)

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/khawjaahmad/nemotron-diarization-web/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/Nemotron-3-Diarization-ONNX)

1. Follow the source guide for the browser front end and the separate ONNX checkpoint. Keep model downloads outside the application source.
2. Select fp32 for WebGPU or a supported WASM variant. Check the selected backend and model status before loading any sensitive audio.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/khawjaahmad/nemotron-diarization-web/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/Nemotron-3-Diarization-ONNX)

1. Load a short audio file with at least one overlapping turn and retain the source.
2. Compare displayed speaker changes with the recording and check whether speakers remain consistent after silence.
3. Carry verified turns into a separate edited transcript. Speaker segmentation alone does not generate transcript words.

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/khawjaahmad/nemotron-diarization-web/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/nvidia/Nemotron-3-Diarization) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/onnx-community/Nemotron-3-Diarization-ONNX)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Source gates do not establish accuracy on your footage. Stylized characters, occluded joints and large kicks can require substantial cleanup.

### How it uses AI

Pinned local pose and segmentation models feed an editable rig-pose payload. The project also describes a video path and separately imports and retargets motion assets from BVH or FBX. [Source](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

### Introduction

Pinned local pose and segmentation models feed an editable rig-pose payload. The project also describes a video path and separately imports and retargets motion assets from BVH or FBX. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

### What it is good for

Block a character pose from an authorized reference photo, inspect joint confidence and apply a corrected first take to an existing Blender armature. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer benchmarks joint and retargeting accuracy on selected images and motion fixtures. The release documentation reports failure cases for stylized line art and extreme poses. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

1. Use the documented source environment with ONNX inference support and the pinned model files.
2. Package the add-on for Install from Disk as described by the project. Use a Blender version within the documented route.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

1. Export the target armature mapping and use a well-lit reference pose on an authorized source.
2. Generate the pose payload, inspect role-map confidence and load it onto the armature in Blender.
3. Compare the reference and viewport, fix unsupported joints, then key a corrected pose before retargeting a longer clip.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Documented Python inference engine with ONNX and the host Blender add-on. The manifest targets Blender 4.2+ with maintainer gates on 5.1.
- **Platforms:** Source-based Blender route. The author does not provide a complete platform support or hardware matrix for the scoped flow.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The source grant does not govern third-party inference weights, rig files or reference media. Check those items independently.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** No paid service is required for documented local pose inference. Model downloads and Blender compute are separate.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

### Limitations

Source gates do not establish accuracy on your footage. Stylized characters, occluded joints and large kicks can require substantial cleanup. [Source 1](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/MOTION_LIBRARY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/xenoaitham/riggermortis/blob/main/docs/BENCHMARKS.md)

Pretrained sound event localization and detection networks estimate event labels and directions from multichannel recordings, with published fine-tuning paths. Prototype a short interactive audiovisual scene with controlled reference states and inspect continuity after a user action. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

### Demo & examples

The maintainers show text-driven interactive examples and document a distinction between the released Fast runtime and model results from the broader research report. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

1. Read both the inference and director package instructions. Establish the selected licensed video checkpoint and a compatible Linux GPU host.
2. Configure the separate Python 3.12 director and any external language or image services needed by the scenario. Start with confirmed sample assets.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

1. Define a small original scene with one observable state change.
2. Review the plan and inspect the short generated continuation. Check whether actor, environment and audio remain coherent after the change.
3. Keep editable state and input references alongside the selected clip and source version.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Murea-Fast inference service with pinned runtime and selected video checkpoint. The optional director web or CLI path uses Python 3.12 and configured model APIs.
- **Platforms:** Linux CUDA host for the published Fast inference path. The developer reports checks on one and four H20 GPUs. The director has a separate Python setup.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 5](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

- **Code:** Apache-2.0
- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 project code is separate from the LTX-2.x Community License governing the Murea-Fast weights. Included examples and third-party components have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by Apache-2.0. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Local inference needs suitable compute. The selected plan and image providers are separately billed if used.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

### Limitations

Maintainer demos and timing do not establish real-time behavior for the intended scene or all workloads. A scene’s authored world-state representation can be preserved independently of the rendered clip. [Source 1](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/inference/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Murea-Labs/InWorld/blob/main/agent/README.md) [Source 4](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

### Get the tool

- [Documentation](https://huggingface.co/MureaLabs/InWorld)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. The v0.1 pre-release fixes a narrow edit profile. Model load and first kernel compilation take time. The maintained example does not establish output consistency on other clips.

### How it uses AI

A ComfyUI node submits video frames and edit instructions to an upstream JoyAI model in a configured worker and receives a corresponding edited frame sequence. [Source](https://github.com/hiroki-abe-58/ComfyUI-JoyAI-Video-Edit/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/hiroki-abe-58/ComfyUI-JoyAI-Video-Edit/releases/tag/v0.1.0) [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03974) [Source](https://huggingface.co/jdopensource/JoyAI-Video-Edit/resolve/main/README.md)

### Introduction

Compare an instruction-based edit to a short landscape clip and inspect continuity before using the result in a finishing timeline. ### How it uses AI

Optional RapidOCR ONNX reads scanned PDF pages. Vision-language captioning can add an image description with nearby text and caption context, while raw extracted artifacts remain separate. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/rc-docparser/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/project/rc-docparser/0.2.2/)

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/LICENSE-APACHE/raw?ref=HEAD)

1. Open the browser application in a modern browser. Permit the camera only for live capture, or import page photos instead.
2. For the source route, follow the documented Rust/WebAssembly browser build. Use the browser edition for the reviewed workflow.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/LICENSE-APACHE/raw?ref=HEAD)

1. Capture or import the pages of a short printed handout. Check perspective and corner placement in the preview.
2. Review the English OCR against headings and details, especially proper names and dates.
3. Reorder pages, export a searchable PDF, and verify the text selection before keeping it in an archive.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/LICENSE-APACHE/raw?ref=HEAD)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Modern browser with required camera permission for live input. Vendored Tesseract.js model assets. The source-build toolchain uses Rust and WebAssembly.
- **Platforms:** Current use is the browser route for phone or computer. The project describes Android and iOS native packaging as in progress.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/LICENSE-MIT/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 3](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/LICENSE-APACHE/raw?ref=HEAD)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** Complete dual MIT/Apache software grants were reviewed. Tesseract components, scanned source material and privacy rights follow their own terms.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** No account is required for the reviewed browser workflow. The application stores scans locally in that browser.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/LICENSE-APACHE/raw?ref=HEAD)

### Limitations

OCR is English-only. Browser storage can be cleared or evicted. The maintainer recommends exported PDFs for durable copies, and no native mobile setup is asserted. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/87367647/repository/files/LICENSE-APACHE/raw?ref=HEAD)

## Video Editor Workflow

Video, animation & film · Editing, captions & post-production · Editable transcripts, captions and chapters

First complete guide in the library. The publication and documentation dates are not necessarily the project’s launch date.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Make an editable Portuguese interview or tutorial cut, with camera and screen layers retained for a later NLE finishing pass.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-24. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/LICENSE/raw?ref=HEAD)
- **Creative results · documented:** The maintainer documents a long-form route with a trim-ready OTIO and produces separate captions and chapter data. The short route instead renders a vertical video through HyperFrames. - **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The MIT grant applies to project code. Speech models, Claude service, plug-ins, imported audio and rendered graphics have separate requirements. Open-source workflow. Account access, model usage and optional rendered assets follow the chosen services and local compute. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. The supplied setup prompt covers multiple third-party dependencies. Versioned dependency lists and project checks are documentation evidence. Verify typography, retimed media and overlay alpha in the destination editor.

### How it uses AI

WhisperX word-level timings and a Claude Code editing workflow prepare an OTIO rough cut, optional HyperFrames graphics and timed captions from raw dialogue. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

### Introduction

WhisperX word-level timings and a Claude Code editing workflow prepare an OTIO rough cut, optional HyperFrames graphics and timed captions from raw dialogue. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

### What it is good for

Make an editable Portuguese interview or tutorial cut, with camera and screen layers retained for a later NLE finishing pass. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer documents a long-form route with a trim-ready OTIO and produces separate captions and chapter data. The short route instead renders a vertical video through HyperFrames. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

1. Read the Portuguese platform setup guide and configure the published WhisperX environment, FFmpeg/FFprobe and Node route.
2. Use the named CUDA dependency lock for supported NVIDIA machines or the documented CPU configuration on Mac. Configure the separate assistant account for language-directed editing.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

1. Choose a short representative interview and agree on one rough-cut goal.
2. Check the transcript, time ranges and proposed edits. Create the OTIO interchange and keep any layered caption or graphic files separate.
3. Import the timeline into the finishing editor and verify relinking, picture-in-picture alignment and chapter timings.

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/README.md/raw?ref=HEAD) [Source 2](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects/88327213/repository/files/INSTALL_PROMPT.md/raw?ref=HEAD)

- **Software:** Git, FFmpeg/FFprobe, Node.js 22+, Python 3.11 environment via uv, WhisperX and the separately configured Claude Code route. HyperFrames is used for graphics and vertical rendering.
- **Platforms:** Windows through Git Bash and documented NVIDIA path, CPU transcription on macOS and a Linux route are described. Confirm the selected NLE imports OTIO.

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. ## NeuralSheet

Audio, music & voice · Editable humming and melody arrangement · Optical notation and score editing

First complete guide in the library. The publication and documentation dates are not necessarily the project’s launch date.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Transcribe authorized music for noncommercial study or arrangement and hand corrected notes to a notation editor.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-24. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The developer shows a piano roll filling progressively during transcription. The documented project workflow keeps timing and velocity edits and exports MIDI, MusicXML or a score PDF. [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/releases/tag/v1.2.0) [Source](https://neural-sheet.quassum.com/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DamRsn/muscriptor-gguf)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/releases/tag/v1.2.0) [Source](https://neural-sheet.quassum.com/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DamRsn/muscriptor-gguf)
- **Maintenance and support · documented:** The current v1.2.0 release is available alongside the source and project issue tracker. [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/releases/tag/v1.2.0)
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The app code is Apache-2.0. Its documented MuScriptor weights are CC BY-NC 4.0, restricting commercial use of that model route. Local application and documented weights have no per-run service fee. The noncommercial model restriction still applies. [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/releases/tag/v1.2.0) [Source](https://neural-sheet.quassum.com/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DamRsn/muscriptor-gguf)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Developer speed readings apply to the identified hardware and checkpoint. Correct pitches, rhythm and instrument attribution by listening before export.

### How it uses AI

MuScriptor runs on local Metal kernels and turns a recording into editable note and instrument tracks. Optional Demucs separation routes the stems to independent transcriptions. [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/bring-shrubbery/neural-sheet/releases/tag/v1.2.0) [Source](https://neural-sheet.quassum.com/) [Source](https://huggingface.co/DamRsn/muscriptor-gguf)

1. Choose the current notarized macOS release for Apple Silicon or the documented Xcode source build.
2. Open the editor and select an input device or audio file. Verify the license before fetching the selected local MuScriptor checkpoint.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. 1. Start with a short recording and choose likely instruments. Transcribe and listen against the original.
2. Correct split, missing and doubled notes in the editor and set bar alignment and tempo.
3. Export MIDI or MusicXML and inspect playback and notation independently of the source mix.

### How it uses AI

The Nuke gizmo fits projection geometry from a plate using MoGe depth and optional SAM masks. Blender builds scene data that is placed using the shot camera and tracking inputs. 1. Download the release matching the operating system and architecture. Review its platform signing and media-tool notes.
2. Provision FFmpeg and FFprobe using the documented platform route. Use the pinned source-build prerequisites on Ubuntu.
3. In General settings, choose the editing agent and the transcription provider. The local default model downloads on first use.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/Forward-Future/scarlet/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Forward-Future/scarlet/releases/tag/v0.1.539) [Source 3](https://github.com/Forward-Future/scarlet/blob/main/docs/transcription-models.md)

1. Import a short interview with a clean dialogue track and transcribe it.
2. Select a pause or repeated line in the transcript and ask the agent for a narrower cut.
3. Audition the before and after join in context. Keep or revise it and verify the retained captions.
4. Export an NLE handoff and check clip boundaries, media relinking and caption placement in the chosen finishing app.

The project supplies authored-caption and rendered-speech benchmarks. The in-progress generalization suite uses pinned clips across several edits. These are developer-run evaluations. [Source 1](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/benchmarks/auto-edit/v3/README.md)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/benchmarks/auto-edit/v3/README.md)

1. Read the source-build guide for Windows or Linux. Provision its Rust workspace and the documented pinned FFmpeg toolchain.
2. Launch the desktop editor from the built source. Configure a supported authenticated agent CLI if agent editing is needed.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/benchmarks/auto-edit/v3/README.md)

1. Import a short licensed dialogue clip and make a manual baseline timeline.
2. Use one scoped agent request for pauses or caption timing and inspect each timeline change.
3. Check the rendered dialogue, text and cut intervals against the source before output.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/benchmarks/auto-edit/v3/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Documented Rust/egui native build, FFmpeg and a selected agent CLI. The project documents NeMo speech assets for its available transcription route.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 x64 and Linux x86-64 with glibc 2.28 or newer. No native Mac path is documented in the reviewed setup.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. - **Weights:** The code grant is separate from FFmpeg components, model weights, agent CLI terms and footage rights.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by GPL-3.0. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Source build has no project charge. An authenticated assistant CLI can need an account, subscription or separately billed provider.

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source 1](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/CanadaApollo6/Kinewright/blob/main/benchmarks/auto-edit/v3/README.md)

## CozyClay

3D, reconstruction & assets · Motion capture & character animation · Storyboarding, narrative & comics · Agent-assisted editable production

v3.3.0 adds a model-written motion specification with deterministic frame construction and contact checking, aimable lights, revised camera rails and AI-video reference packs. Storyboard projects were removed.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Stage a rough shot with one performer, a handled prop and a dolly rail, then preserve an editable previs before requesting a separate generated-video pass.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-24. No local execution implied.

### How it uses AI

The v3.3 motion writer uses a separately selected model to produce a motion specification. A deterministic scene engine creates the frames and reports contacts, balance, foot slide and intersections for the writer’s review. ## FreeClipMoCap

Motion capture & character animation · Avatars, digital humans & lip sync · Puppetry and retargeted performance · Games & production pipelines · Mobile, edge & on-device creation

The source repository was created on 2026-11-04. This is its first complete guide in the library.

### Quality assessment: Experimental

Creative use and the code grant are documented. Check-level gaps remain before a recommendation.

Scope: Create a first character-animation take from a phone performance and clean up exported game motion without redrawing every pose.

Evidence checked: 2026-11-24. No local execution implied.

Why Experimental: The current roadmap states that packaging is still in progress and tells users to build the extension archive locally. https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Reviewed the complete project software grant and its redistribution conditions. Model, data and service terms are scoped separately. [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The workflow documents a tracked-skeleton overlay, a camera comparison view and a keyed rig take. The documentation describes where one-camera depth and finger estimates go wrong. [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** The installation and first-use steps are in the cited documentation. Reproduction of this workflow remains unverified. [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** The reviewed sources do not establish workflow-specific support, fixes and unresolved blockers.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use of the exact workflow was not established in this source review.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The complete GPL license covers extension code. Bundled MediaPipe, model files and related dependencies carry their own terms. No project fee or inference API is listed. Use your own footage and compute. [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

Source and documentation review. No hands-on execution by AI Media Scout. Packaging remains in progress. One camera handles depth and roll poorly. Small hands or motion blur can produce unstable fingers. The maintainer discloses MediaPipe usage reports to Google.

### How it uses AI

MediaPipe body and hand trackers derive landmarks from video. The Blender extension solves them onto an armature and caches the landmarks alongside source footage. [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

### Introduction

MediaPipe body and hand trackers derive landmarks from video. The Blender extension solves them onto an armature and caches the landmarks alongside source footage. [Source 1](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

### What it is good for

Create a first character-animation take from a phone performance and clean up exported game motion without redrawing every pose. [Source 1](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

### Demo & examples

The workflow documents a tracked-skeleton overlay, a camera comparison view and a keyed rig take. The documentation describes where one-camera depth and finger estimates go wrong. [Source 1](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

1. Use Blender 5.2+ on Windows x64 and author the extension ZIP using the project’s documented build route.
2. In Blender Preferences open Get Extensions and install from disk. Open the FreeClipMoCap tab in the viewport sidebar.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

1. Film one performer at full height with a stationary camera. Import the clip and select a Rigify or Mixamo armature.
2. Review the proposed bone map, capture landmarks and scrutinize low-confidence hands in the overlay.
3. Solve a short frame range. Check the fitted camera view and adjust feet or hands with keyable IK targets.
4. Export the reviewed take as FBX or BVH, keeping the original clip and keyed action.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/PRIVACY.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/rnsfh/FreeClipMoCap/blob/main/Docs/FILMING.md)

- **Software:** Blender 5.2 or newer. The ZIP bundles MediaPipe, OpenCV, matplotlib and tracking models.
- **Platforms:** Windows 64-bit is the current documented build route.

### Why it merits attention

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. [Source](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/releases/tag/v0.2.2) [Source](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/package.json) [Source](https://ludo.ai/)

### Introduction

The open-source game asset CLI can submit configured model generation and includes local post-processing for spritesheets, animation timing, geometry and engine handoff. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/releases/tag/v0.2.2) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/package.json) [Source 4](https://ludo.ai/)

### What it is good for

Review a game sprite animation and prepare it for an engine by comparing loop seams, aligning frames and retaining editable source assets. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/releases/tag/v0.2.2) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/package.json) [Source 4](https://ludo.ai/)

### Demo & examples

The documentation distinguishes local frame and loop inspection from generation and repair tasks that use hosted models. It describes structured exports for game engines. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/releases/tag/v0.2.2) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/package.json) [Source 4](https://ludo.ai/)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/releases/tag/v0.2.2) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/package.json) [Source 4](https://ludo.ai/)

1. Use Node.js 20+ and the published CLI package. Use local toolbox commands without configuring a hosted account when only editing existing assets.
2. If generating new assets, configure the separately governed Ludo account and choose a budget and model route.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/releases/tag/v0.2.2) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/package.json) [Source 4](https://ludo.ai/)

1. Use an authorized sprite sheet. Inspect the grid and frame timing and compare the first and last frames.
2. Apply only needed edits, then check pivot and frame offsets before building the export.
3. Retain the original art and settings alongside the packed assets and test import in the chosen engine.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/releases/tag/v0.2.2) [Source 3](https://github.com/Ludo-AI/ludo-cli/blob/main/package.json) [Source 4](https://ludo.ai/)

- **Software:** Node.js 20+ and versioned CLI. Generation needs Ludo service access. Toolbox and several file operations run locally.
- **Platforms:** Node CLI. The source does not establish a measured per-OS performance matrix for each tool.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. - **Weights:** MIT covers CLI source and excludes the service’s trademark. Model access, generated assets and third-party export consumers carry separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Project code is governed by MIT. Confirm selected model, source-media and output restrictions separately.
- **Cost:** Local toolbox use requires no generation credit. Hosted generation consumes separately priced credits.

The creative workflow is grounded in the cited documentation. Output quality and compatibility are not hands-on verified. Propose a brief montage from multiple finished or raw clips, then manually re-cut and add a timed voiceover. [Source 1](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/package.json)

### Demo & examples

The maintainer describes copying source references and tags for the assistant to return structured segment ranges. The editor retains a media library, sequence and editable audio layout. [Source 1](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/package.json)

### Install

Follow the documented route for the scoped workflow. The steps below are source references. [Source 1](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/package.json)

1. Use the documented Node 18+ source setup and its FFmpeg route.
2. Open the local editor. Set up the selected Claude Code or other suitable assistant workflow for proposed cuts.
### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. [Source 1](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/package.json)

1. Import short clips and label their useful content.
2. Ask for one montage with a specified duration and review the returned in/out points before importing them.
3. Adjust the order and timing, preview the soundtrack and export a local MP4. Keep the timeline JSON with the media.
### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. [Source 1](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/gitmir-hello/video-montage/blob/main/package.json)

- **Hardware:** Minimum RAM, VRAM and disk space for the scoped workflow are undocumented.
- **Software:** Node.js 18+, browser client, local backend and ffmpeg-static. Node 24 is the documented development environment.
- **Platforms:** Source-based local browser workflow. The source review did not establish a tested per-OS matrix.

The full software license was reviewed. Dependencies and creative assets carry their own terms. ### First project

A small first project makes the input, model output and handoff easy to inspect. ### Hardware & software

Platform and compute constraints refer to the selected route. - **Software:** Bundled FFmpeg/FFprobe, COLMAP and Brush are documented for selected builds. Reconstruction and training acceleration depend on the platform, GPU and driver.
- **Platforms:** Windows 11 x64, Apple Silicon macOS 15+ alpha and Ubuntu 24.04 x86-64 alpha are documented in the exact release.

### How it uses AI

A C++ implementation of a trained reconstruction model converts several ordinary views into per-pixel Gaussians using ggml on CPU or Vulkan.
